A grown-up-sized long-sleeved, ankle-length, button-front union-suit style onesie, hoodless, with a ribbed collar, with simple wrist cuffs and no attached gloves, and with sewn-in bootie feet, in stretch cotton-blend jersey, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a glossy all-over digital print, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of a 1950s greaser. No blank jersey shows, the printed outfit runs from the neckline down the arms and legs to the cuffs, and over the hood if there is one. The print shows a leather jacket over a white T-shirt, jeans, boots printed on the feet, and the sleeves end in plain cuffs with no hands printed. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. No background colour or plain jersey shows around the print, on the shoulders, sleeves or legs. Every sleeve and every leg of the suit is a tube covered completely, all the way round, in the outfit's cloth for that limb (sleeve cloth, trouser, tights or leg fabric), so no grey, blue or coloured background shows on the arms, legs, sides or back. Where the outfit leaves arms or legs bare, the suit itself is cut sleeveless, short-sleeved or short-legged at that point, so the wearer's own limbs show; no printed arm, leg, hand, foot or skin appears on the suit's fabric. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the suit is outfit cloth. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided suit shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. Everything is flat ink on opaque stretch jersey, lace, fur, metal, leather and sequins are printed patterns, never real raised or see-through material. The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth. It may also show flat printed belly hair on __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ showing at a bare midriff or between shirt buttons, all as flat ink. Printed shadows and fold lines suggest the garments, but the suit itself is smooth, flat and uniform in material. It is a complete costume of 1950s greaser in one piece, shown full length from collar to ankles, with a leather jacket over a white T-shirt, jeans on the legs, boots (as the feet), a comb sewn or attached to the suit. The upper half carries the top and the leg tubes carry the lower garment, so the whole outfit reads at once. Sewn-on novelty extras, kept to the edges so the print stays unbroken, glittery elastic cuffs, contrast-colour ribbed cuffs and ankle bands and soft terry-loop cuff trim. It is a bright, playful novelty onesie that is fun to touch.

An easy-fitting adult-sized long-sleeved, short-legged romper-style, drop-hood zip-front onesie, with an attached hood, with open cuffs and bare hands, and with bare calves and ankles, in velour, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a lightly distressed worn-in print, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of a Scottish piper. The outfit's design is wrapped around the entire suit as one continuous print from the collar to the ankles, wrapping around the sides to the back. The print shows a velvet jacket with a plaid, a kilt, with a feather bonnet printed on the hood, with no footwear shown because the suit is footless, and the sleeves end in plain cuffs with no hands printed. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the outfit looks from behind. No background colour or plain jersey shows around the print, on the shoulders, sleeves or legs. The outfit's cloth wraps the whole suit like a pattern, with the body, the outside and inside of each sleeve and each leg, and the sides, so there are no plain coloured side panels or background patches anywhere. Where the outfit leaves arms or legs bare, the suit itself is cut sleeveless, short-sleeved or short-legged at that point, so the wearer's own limbs show; no printed arm, leg, hand, foot or skin appears on the suit's fabric. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided suit shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The suit is solid and opaque and nothing is real but the jersey, textures and sheens are printed pictures only. No standing figure, torso, arms or legs are depicted; the suit's own shape is the body, and the print is only the outfit's fabric covering it from edge to edge. The printed top of a kilt reaching the hem and ending there. The print runs continuously across the front, back, sleeves, legs and hood without a break at the seams. A full Scottish piper costume, head to toe in a single suit, with a velvet jacket with a plaid, a kilt on the legs, a feather bonnet (as the hood), shoes (as the feet), bagpipes sewn or attached to the suit. Nothing of the outfit is missing and the legs of the suit show the lower garment.
